This material is needed only for the current\n44\t  change-definition context. The agent SHALL retain it in the conversation\n45\t  or, at its discretion, in a temporary location outside the repository.\n46\t  It SHALL NOT be persisted in the repository. The agent chooses whether a\n47\t  temporary file is needed and, if so, its filename and format.\n48\t\n49\t**Actions:**\n50\t\n51\t1. Read the change request and existing clarifications.\n52\t2. Draft one statement describing the observable result for the intended\n53\t   human, agent, or system. Include the conditions under which it is needed.\n54\t3. Present the statement to the requester. When the proposed scope is also\n55\t   clear, present its inclusions, exclusions, and constraints in the same\n56\t   exchange. Obtain confirmation of both before drafting dependent definitions.\n57\t4. Ask the requester to resolve any missing information or competing\n58\t   interpretations that would change the outcome.\n59\t5. Revise the statement using the feedback. Repeat the exchange until the\n60\t   requester confirms the wording. Reuse an existing confirmation of the\n61\t   same wording.\n62\t6. Retain the confirmed statement and the confirmation as transient working\n63\t   material.\n64\t\n65\t**Harness command:** None.\n66\t\n67\t**Completion:** One intended-outcome statement is confirmed by the requester\n68\tand available for the following steps. This confirmation does not approve a\n69\tformal artifact or authorize implementation.\n70\t\n71\t**Later use:** The statement guides scope definition in [Define the limits of the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#define-the-limits-of-the-change) and artifact\n72\tselection in [Identify the existing artifacts that apply to the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#identify-the-existing-artifacts-that-apply-to-the-change). It supplies the outcome for a missing intent (INT) drafted\n73\tin [Draft any missing definitions](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#draft-any-missing-definitions) or an amendment proposed in [Propose any needed amendments to existing definitions](AMEND_DEFINITIONS.md#propose-any-needed-amendments-to-existing-definitions). Any lasting definition belongs\n74\tin those formal artifacts.\n75\t\n76\t### Define the limits of the change\n77\t\n78\t**Inputs:** The confirmed outcome from [Describe the intended outcome](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#describe-the-intended-outcome) and the constraints supplied\n79\tby the requester.\n80\t\n81\t**Output:**\n82\t\n83\t- **Formal artifacts:** None created or modified.\n84\t- **Transient working material:** The confirmed scope statement and requester\n85\t  clarification. Retain these under the same conditions as [Describe the intended outcome](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#describe-the-intended-outcome).\n86\t\n87\t**Actions:**\n88\t\n89\t1. List the behaviors, components, and interfaces included in the change.\n90\t2. List the adjacent behaviors, components, and interfaces excluded from it.\n91\t3. List the conditions the result must respect, such as compatibility or a\n92\t   named operating environment.\n93\t4. Use component names where exact file paths are not yet known.\n94\t5. Reuse an existing\n96\t   confirmation when the boundaries are unchanged, including confirmation\n97\t   obtained with the intended outcome. Retain both confirmations even when\n98\t   one reply supplies them.\n99\t7. Retain the confirmed scope as transient working material.\n100\t\n101\t**Harness command:** None.\n102\t\n103\t**Completion:** One scope statement defines what is included, what is\n104\texcluded, and which constraints apply. No unresolved boundary affects the\n105\tdefinitions to be drafted next.\n106\t\n107\t**Later use:** The scope selects artifacts in [Identify the existing artifacts that apply to the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#identify-the-existing-artifacts-that-apply-to-the-change), limits definition work\n108\tin [Draft any missing definitions](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#draft-any-missing-definitions), [Propose any needed amendments to existing definitions](AMEND_DEFINITIONS.md#propose-any-needed-amendments-to-existing-definitions), [Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified), and supplies the work order (WO) boundaries in [Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements](DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements).\n109\t\n110\t### Identify the existing artifacts that apply to the change\n111\t\n112\t**Inputs:** The confirmed outcome, scope limits, any artifact IDs supplied\n113\tin the request, and the existing formal records. Use\n114\t`docs/engineering/README.md` when locating a domain.\n115\t\n116\t**Output:**\n117\t\n118\t- **Formal artifacts:** None created or modified.\n119\t- **Transient working material:** A list of selected artifacts (the artifact\n120\t  inventory), a list of missing definitions, and the evaluator results.\n121\t  These are needed only for the current change-definition context. The agent\n122\t  SHALL retain them in the conversation or, at its discretion, in a temporary\n123\t  location outside the repository. They SHALL NOT be persisted in the\n124\t  repository. The agent chooses whether a temporary file is needed and, if\n125\t  so, its filename and format.\n126\t\n127\t**Actions:**\n128\t\n129\t1. Run the repository inspection command below.\n130\t2. If the request selects a work order (WO), run the selected-work command\n131\t   below. Read that work order (WO) and every file in the returned\n132\t   `context.reading_manifest`.\n133\t3. Otherwise, locate the artifacts named in the request. If none are named,\n134\t   use `docs/engineering/README.md` to locate the domain covering the function\n135\t   or component in the scope. Read its intents (INT), capabilities (CAP),\n136\t   and requirements (REQ). Record a missing domain if none covers the change.\n137\t4. Select the requirements (REQ) whose behavior the change would modify or\n138\t   reuse. Trace each one back to its capabilities (CAP) and intents (INT).\n139\t5. Read the specifications (SPEC), applicable architectures (ARCH), architecture\n140\t   decisions (ADR), verification contracts (VER), and work orders (WO) linked\n141\t   to those selected requirements (REQ). Use the declared relations listed\n142\t   in [Definition links](DEFINITION_LINKS.md#links-between-definitions). Keep unrelated work outside the selected scope.\n143\t6. Read decisions (DEC) and risks (RISK) that name the selected artifacts.\n144\t7. For each selected artifact, retain its name and code, such as Requirement\n145\t   (REQ), its ID, file path, current lifecycle state, and why it applies to the\n146\t   change. Keep this list as transient working material; do not create a\n147\t   repository inventory file.\n148\t8. Assign one treatment to each entry: `reuse`, `amend`, or `reference only`.\n149\t   Use `reuse` only when its existing meaning covers the change unchanged.\n150\t9. List missing definitions by type and purpose. Identify their intended domain.\n151\t\n152\t**Harness commands:**\n153\t\n154\tRepository inspection, used in action 1:\n155\t\n156\t```text\n157\tharnessctl inspect REPO --json\n158\t```\n159\t\n160\tSelected-work inspection, used in action 2. Replace `WO-ID` with the actual\n161\twork order (WO) ID:\n162\t\n163\t```text\n164\tharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\n165\t```\n166\t\n167\tBoth commands are read-only. `inspect` does not select the change scope.\n168\tThe checkpoint-free `check` reports lifecycle context without evaluating\n169\tcheckpoint gates. Preserve its reported blockers and next action.\n170\t\n171\t**Completion:** One inventory identifies the existing artifacts to reuse,\n172\tamend, or consult and the definitions that are missing. Ambiguous artifact\n173\tidentities are resolved before dependent drafting.\n174\t\n175\t**Later use:** The inventory controls creation in [Draft any missing definitions](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#draft-any-missing-definitions), amendments in [Propose any needed amendments to existing definitions](AMEND_DEFINITIONS.md#propose-any-needed-amendments-to-existing-definitions),\n176\tand the verification, risk, decision, and work-order work in [Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified), [Record risks (RISK) affecting the change](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#record-risks-risk-affecting-the-change), [Record unresolved decisions (DEC)](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#record-unresolved-decisions-dec), [Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements](DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements).\n177\t\n178\t## Read next when\n179\t\n180\tDefinitions are missing → [DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#procedure](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#procedure). They may support\\nor be referenced by formal artifacts but do not grant authority by themselves.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"47268c9ec1e3551fb547ad3d1ef7b8da0b773b52b3a4364f42c36cc64f98bf83\"}, {\"heading\": \"artifact-locations\", \"title\": \"Artifact locations\", \"anchor\": \"artifact-locations\", \"start_line\": 63, \"end_line\": 96, \"content\": \"## Artifact locations\\n\\nNew formal artifacts use these directories below\\n`docs/engineering/DOMAIN/`. `DOMAIN` is the selected domain name. --json`\\nreports whether one supported draft has an admissible shape. Draft admission grants no authority to write or approve.\\n\\nChoose the least complex design that satisfies the agreed requirements and\\nconstraints. Justify material added complexity by the need it serves and why\\na simpler approach is insufficient. Consider the effort to understand, build,\\ntest, operate and change the whole solution. Preserve correctness and the project's required quality and\\nsafety properties.\\n\\nStart from the smallest complete solution.   "tool_use_id": "toolu_014mvdeNuDtXnZ6xu2iiXEDD",

  "content": "1\t# Artifact Authoring\n2\t\n3\tThe key words **MUST**, **MUST NOT**, **REQUIRED**, **SHALL**, **SHALL NOT**,\n4\t**SHOULD**, **SHOULD NOT**, **RECOMMENDED**, **NOT RECOMMENDED**, **MAY**, and\n5\t**OPTIONAL** in this document are to be interpreted as described in BCP 14\n6\t(RFC 2119 and RFC 8174) when, and only when, they appear in all capitals.\n7\t\n8\t## Purpose and precedence\n9\t\n10\tThis policy guides authoring, design and review. Templates and skills point\n11\there; `harnessctl create-artifact` prints the selected type's checklist.\n12\tThe shared principles and questions are maintained here.\n13\t\n14\tIt ranks below the machine contracts, approved formal semantics, and the\n15\tworkflow, decision-right, quality-gate, and traceability policies, and above\n16\ttemplates, skills, prompts, and model prose. It grants no authority: a\n17\tchecklist item is a review standard, not an approval. Rules marked\n18\t*mechanical* are enforced by the validator or a gate; the rest are judgement\n19\trules for the accountable reviewer.\n20\t\n21\t## Design simplicity\n22\t\n23\tFor unfinished work, `harnessctl validate-draft REPO --artifact ID --json`\n24\treports whether one supported draft has an admissible shape. Only exact values\n25\tat the same type and field in this evaluator's canonical template, and missing\n26\tor empty required authoring links, can be reported as incomplete. Invalid\n27\tidentity, dates, metadata shapes, actual endpoints, lifecycle history and\n28\tdisposition are errors. A missing non-template ID is an error. Background\n29\tfindings remain visible; this result makes no whole-repository validity claim.\n30\tComplete the authoring checklist and pass ordinary approval gates before\n31\trequesting approval. Draft admission grants no authority to write or approve.\n32\t\n33\tChoose the least complex design that satisfies the agreed requirements and\n34\tconstraints. Justify material added complexity by the need it serves and why\n35\ta simpler approach is insufficient. Consider the effort to understand, build,\n36\ttest, operate and change the whole solution. Defer speculative needs. Review\n37\tboth the necessity of the specified behavior and the simplicity of its\n38\timplementation. Preserve correctness and the project's required quality and\n39\tsafety properties.\n40\t\n41\tStart from the smallest complete solution. Each project supplies its agreed\n42\tusers, scale, operating conditions and quality constraints. Agreed future needs\n43\tcan matter; imagined flexibility alone is insufficient. Credible risk analysis\n44\tcan justify prevention before an incident. Fewer lines, shorter specifications\n45\tor fewer tests do not establish simplicity; moving work to users, operations\n46\tor dependencies also has a cost.\n47\t\n48\tApply the relevant questions below before definition approval and during design\n49\tand implementation review. Return unjustified obligations or design complexity\n50\tfor revision through the existing decision process. Explain material choices\n51\tin the existing specification, design or review: the need, why a simpler option\n52\tis insufficient and the added cost. Scale the explanation to the decision; a\n53\tsmall choice may need one sentence, a significant design may use its existing\n54\tarchitecture decision. Trivial changes need no separate comparison document.\n55\t\n56\tThese are human review standards. They introduce no separate KISS artifact,\n57\tmandatory score, receipt, automated simplicity gate or additional approval.\n58\t\n59\t## requirement\n60\t\n61\t### Checklist\n62\t\n63\t- State the observable behavior and why it is needed. Ordinary language is accepted; `SHALL` is optional.\n64\t- What agreed outcome does this obligation serve, for whom and under which conditions? Separate requirements from assumptions and speculative needs.\n65\t- Include a concrete acceptance condition in `Examples`, `Acceptance`, `measure`, `verification_notes`, or a linked verification contract.\n66\t- Name the source capability and a verification method. Supported method arrays contain `test`, `analysis`, `inspection`, or `demonstration`; retained text methods remain readable.\n67\t- Replace unfinished placeholders and resolve blocking decisions before approval.\n68\t\n69\t### Guidance\n70\t\n71\tWrite enough for someone to implement and check the behavior. Use the template's\n72\tsections when helpful. Word counts, sentence shapes and numbers of code references\n73\tare not checked. Writing hints never block approval. Optional metadata must still\n74\thave a valid value when supplied.\n75\t\n76\t## intent\n77\t\n78\t### Checklist\n79\t\n80\t- Explain the present problem, who it affects, and the outcome wanted.\n81\t- What is the smallest useful outcome, and which needs and constraints are actually agreed?\n82\t- Say how the owner will know the change helped; an honest baseline may be `not measured`.\n83\t- State the important scope limits. Create a new intent when the desired outcome changes.\n84\t\n85\t## capability\n86\t\n87\t### Checklist\n88\t\n89\t- Say what an actor should be able to do and any relevant conditions.\n90\t- Does each supported case serve an agreed need under those conditions?\n91\t- Link the intent it serves. The graph lists the derived requirements.\n92\t- Put implementation details where they help the implementer; no required `can ... under` wording.\n93\t\n94\t## specification\n95\t\n96\t### Checklist\n97\t\n98\t- Explain what an implementation must do, including meaningful failure behavior.\n99\t- Is each promised behavior necessary? Could a simpler contract meet the agreed need? Prescribe a mechanism only when it is a necessary constraint.\n100\t- Link the requirements and show an example someone can check.\n101\t- Give a rule a stable identifier when evidence or a decision needs to refer to it.\n102\t- Check that supplied rule references exist and identifiers are unambiguous.\n103\t\n104\tMissing summaries and ambiguous rule references may produce draft hints. Hints\n105\thelp a reviewer; they do not withhold approval. Existing approved text need not\n106\tbe rewritten to adopt these checklists.\n107\t\n108\t## architecture\n109\t\n110\t### Checklist\n111\t\n112\t- `addresses` names only architecturally significant requirements; `conforms_to` names the specifications it must respect.\n113\t- Components, responsibilities, dependency direction, and trust boundaries are each stated.\n114\t- The decision assessment is complete: `adr_required` with triggers, or `no_significant_decision` with a rationale.\n115\t- For material added complexity, what simpler approach was considered, including existing capabilities? Explain why it is insufficient and the added cost across development, testing, operation and change.\n116\t\n117\t## adr\n118\t\n119\t### Checklist\n120\t\n121\t- Context, drivers, at least two considered options with consequences, the decision, and its consequences.\n122\t- The chosen option is stated as a decision, not a preference.\n123\t- If the chosen option adds material complexity, why is the simpler option insufficient for the agreed constraints, and is the added cost justified?\n124\t- Every `decides` target architecture is an active artifact.\n125\t\n126\t## verification\n127\t\n128\t### Checklist\n129\t\n130\t- Independence: expected values derive from the requirements and specifications, never from candidate output.\n131\t- A requirement-to-evidence matrix with a pass condition per requirement.\n132\t- Cover normal behavior and meaningful failure outcomes or risks; do not invent failure scenarios to fill a template.\n133\t- What accepted behavior or quality does each test or CI check establish? Cover distinct relevant outcomes, reuse applicable evidence, and avoid mirroring every implementation branch or combination.\n134\t- Pass criteria name the platforms and the evaluator.\n135\t\n136\t## work_order\n137\t\n138\t### Checklist\n139\t\n140\t- `[execution_scope].paths` is exact: files or component-prefix directories, nothing wider.\n141\t- `[assurance]` is classified and its rationale names what later decisions depend on.\n142\t- In scope and out of scope are both stated; the decision envelope says what the implementer may and may not decide.\n143\t- Stop conditions and the completion report format are stated.\n144\t- Is every planned change needed for the agreed outcome? Include removal or adjustment of obsolete behavior, tests and instructions within the approved scope.\n145\t- Inspect supporting paths before approval and assess the supplied plan through [Draft work orders](harness/DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements). Keep reasons in Expected change surface; do not add a separate planning artifact.\n146\t\n147\t## Review of implemented changes\n148\t\n149\t- Does the result satisfy the necessary contract, and did implementation reveal unnecessary specified behavior? Resolve an excessive approved obligation through the existing amendment process before changing its behavior.\n150\t- Is each material addition needed? Could a direct solution or existing capability meet the same constraints with less total work to understand, test, operate and change?\n151\t- Does the normal design or review evidence explain significant complexity and its cost? Remove unused generality or indirection that has no agreed purpose.\n152\t- Do tests and CI establish the accepted behavior and relevant quality properties? Remove obsolete checks together with the obligations they no longer serve.\n153\t\n154\tRetain material findings and their resolution with the ordinary review evidence.\n155\tApplying these questions is part of that review, not a second review or receipt.\n156\t\n157\t## release_contract\n158\t\n159\t### Checklist\n160\t\n161\t- `gates` names every work order the release may include.\n162\t- Version, rollback conditions, and evidence expectations are explicit.\n163\t\n164\t## operating_contract\n165\t\n166\t### Checklist\n167\t\n168\t- `assures` names every requirement it claims continuing assurance for.\n169\t- Observability, support, and operating obligations are measurable.\n170\t\n171\t## decision\n172\t\n173\tA pending question becomes a `decision` (`DEC-`) when it blocks a transition\n174\tof another artifact, concerns more than one artifact, or must survive the\n175\tapproval of the artifact that raised it. Below that threshold the actor asks\n176\tand the answer stays in the transition's `reason`.\n177\t\n178\t### Checklist\n179\t\n180\t- `kind` is `question` (an ambiguity met while authoring or planning) or `deviation` (an implementation cannot meet one rule of one specification). *(mechanical)*\n181\t- One `question`, `raised_by`, at least two `[[options]]` with `id` and `label`, and a `recommendation` naming one option. *(mechanical)*\n182\t- A deviation also names `against = \"SPEC-xxx#PREFIX-AREA-NNN\"`, a rule identifier the specification defines *(mechanical: E-DCM-005)*, and the `observed` fact; its options are drawn from `amend`, `supersede`, `accept`, `stop` and include `stop`. *(mechanical)*\n183\t- `concerns` names every artifact the question is about; `blocks` names the artifacts that cannot change state while it is `open`, each also in `concerns`. *(mechanical)*\n184\t- The `[disposition]` table is written by `harnessctl decide`; a hand-written one is `E-DCM-003`. A deferral needs a scope and a revisit trigger; accepting a deviation needs a revisit trigger. *(mechanical)*\n185\t- `decided` and `withdrawn` decisions are never deleted or rewritten.\n186\t- The definition templates carry no `Open decisions` section: a definition's pending decisions are the `DEC-` artifacts that name it in `blocks`, and the approval gate reads them from the graph. A legacy section, where one remains, reads `None` or lists `DEC-` identifiers; prose there is `E-DCM-004`.\n187\t\n188\t## risk\n189\t\n190\t### Checklist\n191\t\n192\t- Record a description, an owner and a next action.\n193\t- Use `harnessctl raise-risk --description TEXT --action TEXT --owner ROLE` with a domain and title.\n194\t- Stage, category, threatened artifacts and scoring are optional. If scoring is useful, supply likelihood and impact together (1–5); the command computes their product.\n195\t- Request `--with-decision` only when the owner needs a decision that blocks named artifacts. A risk by itself does not block work.\n196\t- Keep earlier scores, decisions and dispositions as recorded; no migration is required.\n197\t\n198\t\n199\t## verification_record and release_record\n200\t\n201\tPrepared by `capture-verification` and `prepare-release`; they are not\n202\tauthored by hand. This policy does not apply to them.\n203\t"
}

  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-scope37-20261011\\\\claude-331\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"c4ffde0efff3a57df41cf6b282896e6827ea289607f8b73faa8f7c09d0c70213\", \"bytes\": 8654, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nDefinitions, work scope and verification requirements are ready for work planning.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"3977586afdaba8e900cfd38bfd72b02ccf3d011048dae4b247fe81a24c64c9cc\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nRead [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#links-from-work-orders-wo](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#links-from-work-orders-wo), [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#complete-work-scope](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#complete-work-scope) and [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). Apply [../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#design-simplicity](../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#design-simplicity) and [../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#work_order](../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#work_order).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"d2a9a7707114a465ebde20884e2a996807663ab4aa94ff11008f2360617efe50\"}, {\"heading\": \"procedure\", \"title\": \"Procedure\", \"anchor\": \"procedure\", \"start_line\": 11, \"end_line\": 143, \"content\": \"## Procedure\\n\\n### Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements\\n\\n**Inputs:** The outcome, scope, artifact inventory, definition drafts,\\namendment proposals, verification contracts (VER), risks (RISK), and\\nunresolved decisions (DEC).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** New or completed work orders (WO) in `draft`.\\n  Applicable risks (RISK) and open decisions (DEC) updated with the actual\\n  work order (WO) links. Lifecycle states and dispositions are preserved.\\n  Approved work orders (WO) remain unchanged; proposed scope changes require\\n  review.\\n- **Transient working material:** The review handoff and evaluator results.\\n  Retain these under the same conditions as [Describe the intended outcome](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#describe-the-intended-outcome).\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Inspect the implementation and follow its callers, references and consumers.\\n   Check tests and fixtures, instructions and documentation, packaging and CI,\\n   and verification outputs where they apply. Keep the working inventory in\\n   the conversation or temporary storage outside the repository.\\n   Group each complete, independently verifiable outcome into one bounded\\n   work order with its necessary supporting changes.\\n2. Select an existing work order (WO) in `draft` for a unit only if its scope\\n   matches. Otherwise, create a new work order (WO) with the commands below.\\n3. Complete each draft using the field table below. In Expected change\\n   surface, name the inspected paths and give a short reason for each.\\n   Explain non-applicable areas and unknown destinations briefly.\\n4. Check that every selected requirement (REQ) has the specification (SPEC)\\n   and verification contract (VER) coverage defined in [Complete work scope](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#complete-work-scope).\\n5. Apply `## work_order` \\u2192 `### Checklist` in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n6. Add the new work order (WO) IDs to applicable `threatens` relations in risks\\n   (RISK) and `concerns` or `blocks` relations in open decisions (DEC). Keep\\n   every blocked ID in `concerns`. Preserve any required pairing between the\\n   risk (RISK) and decision (DEC).\\n7. If the planned change needs a missing release or operating contract,\\n   use [Prepare a release or operating contract](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#prepare-a-release-or-operating-contract) now that the work\\n   order IDs exist. Include its draft in the proposed package.\\n8. Run the validation command below.\\n9. Correct findings within the proposed drafts. Rerun validation after edits.\\n   Report unrelated findings separately. Retain findings that need a decision\\n   or an accepted-definition amendment as unresolved dependencies.\\n10. Run the selected-work check below for each proposed work order (WO).\\n    Supply every planned file from Expected change surface with\\n    `--planned-path`. Inspect `scope.preparation`: resolve uncovered or\\n    invalid paths in the draft before requesting approval. A successful\\n    command exit does not establish coverage or a complete impact analysis.\\n    Explain future generated destinations using\\n    [Generated outputs](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#generated-outputs).\\n11. Present the outcome, scope, artifact IDs and paths, validation results,\\n    unresolved decisions (DEC), and unapplied amendments. Include the\\n    evaluator's reported next action for each selected work order (WO).\\n\\n| Field or section | Content |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| `Objective` | The outcome this work order (WO) will deliver. |\\n| `In scope` / `Out of scope` | The boundaries from [Define the limits of the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#define-the-limits-of-the-change) assigned to this work order (WO). |\\n| `Constraints` | The conditions from [Define the limits of the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#define-the-limits-of-the-change) that apply to this work. |\\n| `Expected change surface` | Inspected planned paths, the reason for each, and unresolved destinations. |\\n| `[execution_scope].paths` | Exact repository-relative files or component directories ending in `/`. Use forward slashes; no wildcards, absolute paths, or `..`. |\\n| `Authorized decision envelope` | What the implementer may decide and what requires another accountable decision. |\\n| `Required verification` / `Evidence to record` | The checks and retained outputs defined in [Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified). |\\n| `Stop and escalate conditions` | The conditions that require pausing this work. |\\n| `Completion report format` | The required report of completed behavior, checks, limitations, and next accountable decision. |\\n| `[assurance]` | `commit_bound_verification`, its `rationale`, and `decided_by`. Apply the classification rules in [Assurance classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). Record the human who confirmed the classification; an agent may propose it and record that human's decision. |\\n| `[relations]` | `implements` \\u2192 requirements (REQ); `specifications` \\u2192 specifications (SPEC); `verification` \\u2192 verification contracts (VER); `architecture` \\u2192 applicable architectures (ARCH) and required architecture decisions (ADR). |\\n\\nFor PR-based work, include the proposed [review-publication grant](AUTHORITY.md#review-publication-authority)\\nin the decision envelope. Name its repository, source branch, target branch\\nand selected work. Include both the ready-record publication and the later\\ndecision-only push in the expected scope. Present that grant explicitly with\\nimplementation approval; do not infer it from ordinary work authority.\\n\\nIf the assurance classification has not been confirmed, present it as a\\nproposal in the review handoff. Report the missing decision. Do not fill\\n`decided_by` with the draft author's name or an example identity to satisfy\\nvalidation. The human may confirm the classification while reviewing the\\nwork order; record that decision before previewing approval.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nFor each new work order (WO), run the creation sequence from [Draft any missing definitions](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#draft-any-missing-definitions).\\nInspect the preview before creation; replace `WO-ID` with its `allocated_id`:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type work_order --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type work_order --id WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nValidate the formal artifacts after completing the drafts and links:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl validate REPO --json\\n```\\n\\nInspect each proposed work order (WO) using its actual ID:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nTo assess the proposed file list, repeat `--planned-path` for every file:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --planned-path PATH --planned-path ANOTHER-PATH --json\\n```\\n\\nThis option is available only when the selected released evaluator supports\\nit. If it is unavailable, report that version gap; do not run candidate\\nsource to govern the repository.\\n\\nThe optional planning view evaluates no checkpoint. It reports explicit\\nscope matches, existing automatic admission, uncovered paths and invalid\\ndeclarations. It does not inspect dependencies for the agent, prove that\\nthe supplied list is complete, or turn planned paths into observed changes.\\nDo not combine it with checkpoint or actual-change inputs.\\n\\nValidation does not approve the change. The checkpoint-free `check` reports\\nlifecycle context without evaluating checkpoint gates.\\n\\n**Completion:** Draft work orders (WO) are presented for review with their\\ngoverning artifacts and evaluator results. Every unresolved dependency is\\nidentified. Preparation does not approve or start execution.\\n\\n**Later use:** The authorization process reviews this artifact package,\\nresolves approval blockers, and applies permitted lifecycle transitions.\\nOnly the formal artifacts carry lasting definitions and work authority;\\ntransient working material is not persisted in the repository.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"cfcd1bbe3d8874c6ed9fdd4f56f01ef65c16562f552fcbf390b7a0a82dd519df\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-scope37-20261011\\\\claude-331\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"892ec2fedefc272f6bc2580268395e487f392cf5d92a3ea81fac015761bc87a8\", \"bytes\": 8641, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"complete-work-scope\", \"title\": \"Complete work scope\", \"anchor\": \"complete-work-scope\", \"start_line\": 47, \"end_line\": 65, \"content\": \"## Complete work scope\\n\\nEvery requirement (REQ) selected by a work order (WO)\\nMUST link back to active capabilities (CAP) and intents (INT). It MUST also\\nhave selected active specifications (SPEC) and verification contracts (VER).\\nReuse existing artifacts where their scope applies.\\n\\nA work order should cover one complete bounded outcome, including its\\nforeseeable tests, documentation and retained evidence. Use exact file paths\\nby default. Use a narrow existing component directory when the affected\\nfiles belong to that component. A matching directory does not authorize\\nunrelated behavior.\\n\\nPrepare this scope through\\n[Draft work orders](DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements).\\nDo not split predictable supporting changes into extra work orders merely\\nbecause they use different file types. A genuine expansion after approval\\nstill needs the applicable human decision; preserve the approved history.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"021ca3cdecffebe415ce9df445c0c48e24a0acde7609d25220c0fb7d470e054e\"}]}]}",
  "is_error": false
}

{

  "type": "tool_result",
  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-scope37-20261011\\\\claude-331\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"892ec2fedefc272f6bc2580268395e487f392cf5d92a3ea81fac015761bc87a8\", \"bytes\": 8641, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"links-from-work-orders-wo\", \"title\": \"Links from work orders (WO)\", \"anchor\": \"links-from-work-orders-wo\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 28, \"content\": \"## Links from work orders (WO)\\n\\nA work order (WO) selects the definitions and verification contracts (VER) that govern\\nits execution. Links to requirements (REQ) alone are not enough.\\n\\n| Source \\u2192 target | Relation | Meaning and coverage |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Requirement (REQ) | `implements` | Names every requirement (REQ) in the authorized work scope. |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Specification (SPEC) | `specifications` | Names the specifications (SPEC) governing those requirements (REQ). |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Architecture (ARCH) or Architecture decision (ADR) | `architecture` | Names every applicable architecture (ARCH) and every required architecture decision (ADR). |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Verification contract (VER) | `verification` | Names the verification contracts (VER) for the work. |\\n\\nFor example, a work order (WO) can declare these links in its metadata. The IDs\\nbelow are examples, not records created by this document.\\n\\n```toml\\n[relations]\\nimplements = [\\\"REQ-EXAMPLE-001\\\"]\\nspecifications = [\\\"SPEC-EXAMPLE-001\\\"]\\nverification = [\\\"VER-EXAMPLE-001\\\"]\\n```\\n\\nThe referenced requirement (REQ) must also link back to a capability (CAP) and intent (INT).\\nAdd architecture (ARCH) links when architecture (ARCH) applies; do not invent them just to\\nfill a field.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e5892787048f79f437b0395f9e1269152ce3d1d1b6fb28994f42849bd12a4f54\"}, {\"heading\": \"assurance-classification\", \"title\": \"Assurance classification\", \"anchor\": \"assurance-classification\", \"start_line\": 86, \"end_line\": 116, \"content\": \"## Assurance classification\\n\\nEach work order (WO) in `approved` or\\n`in_progress` MUST set `assurance.commit_bound_verification` to `required`\\nor `not_required`.\\n\\nUse `required` when later decisions depend on the correctness of changes to\\ncode, required checks, CI configuration, harness policy, artifact definitions,\\nor their links. These are examples of \\u201cchanged trusted state\\u201d: information or\\nbehavior that others will rely on when accepting, releasing, or operating the\\nresult.\\n\\nFor example, a work order (WO) changes the code that validates artifact links.\\nA later release decision relies on that validation being correct. The work\\norder (WO) therefore needs verification tied to its exact candidate commit.\\n\\nUse `not_required` only when the work solely records or transports a\\nverification, release, supersession, publication, or deployment decision\\nthat an authorized human has already made. It does not make a new decision\\nor change the definitions, implementation, or evidence behind that decision.\\n\\nFor example, a work order (WO) commits an already authorized release decision\\nin a release record (RLS), with no change to the released code, scope, or\\nsupporting evidence. The work records the decision; it does not reassess the\\nrelease.\\n\\nA work order (WO) that combines both kinds of work MUST be split or classified\\n`required`. `not_required` removes the obligation to prepare a new\\nverification record (VREC) for that work; it does not remove required checks\\nor the links to verification contracts (VER).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"6ad947ba93cfc84640c82a0b165448ce2c15f04beed36f65a3dc031e4287aab3\"}, {\"heading\": \"generated-outputs\", \"title\": \"Generated outputs\", \"anchor\": \"generated-outputs\", \"start_line\": 66, \"end_line\": 85, \"content\": \"## Generated outputs\\n\\nExisting scope rules admit the selected work order's own file, records that\\ndirectly verify or release that work order, and those records' declared\\nevaluator-evidence paths. This does not admit those records' parent directories.\\nThe selected work order's own packet directory is also already admitted by\\nthe existing scope rules; the planning report identifies that separate rule.\\n\\nList other planned evidence, review and log destinations explicitly in the\\nwork order's scope unless an existing rule covers them. A neighboring file\\nor a record linked only to another work order is not automatically admitted.\\nThe conditional rule for recording risks during execution remains unchanged;\\nit is not a blanket admission of future files during preparation.\\n\\nIf a future record's ID is not allocated, mark its destination as unresolved\\nand explain the relationship rule expected to cover it. Do not create a\\nrecord or invent an ID to complete the plan. Once the evaluator prepares\\nthe record, assess its actual returned record and evidence paths. An\\nunresolved destination is not an already covered file.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"21cb2760e5cf61158c5d19cca2af8dbe727ecf3df32247987cc1636059bde3e9\"}]}]}",
  "is_error": false
}

For context open,\n230\tcontext `before` is null and `after` is zero. Create/revise increments the context\n231\tonce; freeze reports equal context versions. {

  "content": "1\t# Private lifecycle rehearsal wire contract\n2\t\n3\tThe v2 endpoint accepts only `lifecycle-v2.json`. Every request and result selects\n4\ttest data. Git remains authoritative for real work. Existing v1 routes and the\n5\tseven read-only MCP tools keep their contract; they cannot dispatch v2 actions.\n6\tIn an explicitly configured test project their results use\n7\t`se-harness-graph-read/v2`, with a required test-copy label. Ordinary Phase 2\n8\tprojects retain their v1 read result shapes. Revision byte hashing is unchanged;\n9\ttest revisions add explicit test-copy provenance, without rewriting v1 fixtures.\n10\t\n11\t`inspect` accepts check, preflight and validate. `preview` runs a mutation in a\n12\tdiscarded projection and returns its input digest and complete proposed file\n13\teffects. `apply` requires that digest, reruns the released checks, and commits\n14\tthe complete result once. A preview never changes graph state. Actor strings\n15\tare synthetic test inputs, not authenticated human consent.\n16\t\n17\tThe digest binds project/context versions, all selected revision IDs, retained\n18\tsnapshot, exact command/file inputs, principal and released evaluator identity.\n19\tMode and operation key are excluded from the preview binding, but included in\n20\tthe accepted request digest used for principal-scoped retry lookup.\n21\t\n22\t## Permitted file effects\n23\t\n24\t| Action | Permitted effects in the disposable projection |\n25\t| --- | --- |\n26\t| check / preflight / validate | No file writes |\n27\t| transition | Only selected newly authored artifacts and evaluator sidecars declared by their records |\n28\t| decide | Selected new decision and its explicitly linked rehearsal risk |\n29\t| raise-risk | New risk and optional paired decision at released domain paths |\n30\t| handoff | Declared bounded file inputs within the selected work order's scope; released handoff packet and evaluator evidence |\n31\t| capture-verification | New VREC and its released evaluator sidecar |\n32\t| prepare-release | New RLS and its released evaluator sidecar |\n33\t\n34\tHandoff first uses the released procedure's fixed `evidence` preparation command,\n35\tthen `check --checkpoint handoff --from-git`. It exposes no arbitrary evidence\n36\tcommand. File inputs cannot alter formal records, harness selection/instructions,\n37\tGit configuration, hooks, or executables used by the service. The service never\n38\truns source tests. The local qualification runner supplies observed test evidence.\n39\t\n40\tThe adapter enumerates every regular file before and after the operation. It\n41\trejects deletions, links, unexpected paths, missing sidecars, imported-record\n42\tchanges and evaluator failure before opening an acceptance transaction.\n43\t\n44\t## Snapshots and Git\n45\t\n46\tEach accepted operation retains an immutable complete snapshot: exact file bytes,\n47\tself-contained Git history and its source/test-candidate identities. Content\n48\tdigests bind the snapshot to the resulting baseline. Context heads, artifact\n49\trevisions, snapshot and receipt share one database transaction. Temporary Git\n50\trepositories are discarded after the result is accepted or refused.\n51\t\n52\tCapture first commits the exact input projection as test candidate P. P excludes\n53\tthe new VREC. The output retains the input hosted baseline B, source fixture S,\n54\tP and evaluator identity. Actual implementation candidate C is recorded separately\n55\tby qualification. Subsequent commits never alter the candidate stored in a VREC.\n56\t\n57\tExport selects an immutable baseline, checks its complete snapshot and Git bundle,\n58\tand returns exact bounded bytes. The installed client writes only to a new explicit\n59\tdestination and leaves an incomplete marker until every file hash is checked.\n60\tNo export writes to or synchronizes an authoritative repository.\n61\t\n62\t## Bounds and failure categories\n63\t\n64\tKeep 4 MiB requests, 2 MiB responses, 1 MiB individual file inputs, two concurrent\n65\tprojections, 120-second evaluator calls and 10,000 retained artifact revisions.\n66\tA pilot snapshot/export that exceeds the response bound is refused atomically.\n67\tRetained complete snapshots have a separate explicit project count bound of 128.\n68\tThis is a small private pilot, not an unbounded document store.\n69\t\n70\tBoundary/malformed requests, stale inputs, evaluator refusals, invalid output and\n71\tunknown transport outcomes remain distinct. Read the operation receipt after a\n72\tlost reply. A changed request under the same accepted key is a conflict. The\n73\tservice does not automatically rerun a mutation at a newer version.\n74\t\n75\t## Bounded Cypher reads\n76\t\n77\tThe existing `cypher` MCP/HTTP read selects one exact view. Its request uses the\n78\tread schema, not the lifecycle command schema. Read the public labels and\n79\tproperties in [the physical representation](../../docs/notes/hosted-artifact-graph.md#physical-representation)\n80\tbefore selecting a query. `Artifact` and `Revision` are distinct public labels.\n81\t\n82\tThe admitted form has one `MATCH` or `OPTIONAL MATCH` path, labelled nodes,\n83\toptional parameterized property comparisons, `RETURN`, and a numeric `LIMIT`.\n84\t\n85\tRelationship paths are supported. Use `-[edge:TYPE]->` or `<-[edge:TYPE]-` between\n86\tlabelled nodes, with a public relationship type from the physical representation.\n87\tEach node and relationship needs a distinct variable name. Anonymous relationship\n88\tsyntax such as `-[:TYPE]->` is refused; a refusal of that form does not mean\n89\trelationship traversal is unsupported. Return-column aliases (`AS alias`) are\n90\toptional.\n91\t\n92\tFor example, with `parameters` containing a selected artifact ID under `artifact`:\n93\t\n94\t```cypher\n95\tMATCH (r:Revision) WHERE r.artifact_id = $artifact RETURN r.artifact_id AS id, r.status AS status LIMIT 10\n96\t```\n97\t\n98\tUse the response's completeness fields with the requested row/byte/depth budget.\n99\tThis example supplies no project filter: the service binds the selected project\n100\tand view. Unlabelled nodes, `WITH`, aggregation and arbitrary database syntax are\n101\tnot part of this small public read form. A rejected query has not completed a read.\n102\tKeep the exact refusal; consult the documented form before constructing another\n103\trequest. Do not weaken query admission or bypass it through a direct database call.\n104\t"

  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORIZE_WORK.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-scope37-20261011\\\\claude-331\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORIZE_WORK.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"991b526d6280554dcca6901c1790e5519b8894e08635a1f10618aa84a29aaf28\", \"bytes\": 15212, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nThe proposed package needs validation, review or a human authorization decision.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"8b18d925f39f314acc9ea6bcc9820015c4c9430d836144e85bc31b670752371b\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nFor reviewing each definition, use its selected [Type checklist](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#type-checklists). Before review, read [AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights](AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights) and [AUTHORITY.md#delegation-and-separation](AUTHORITY.md#delegation-and-separation). Before approving work, read [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). For an open or deferred decision, read [RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#blocking-decisions](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#blocking-decisions).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"9aa32693606102a5aa74b2e4a614aed6904471e5f618dea93356baac97c86ca2\"}, {\"heading\": \"assess-the-proposed-artifact-package\", \"title\": \"Assess the proposed artifact package\", \"anchor\": \"assess-the-proposed-artifact-package\", \"start_line\": 32, \"end_line\": 83, \"content\": \"### Assess the proposed artifact package\\n\\n**Inputs:** The selected work order (WO) IDs, proposed definitions, validation\\nresults, and existing decision records (DEC).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None created or modified.\\n- **Transient working material:** A review list identifying the selected\\n  artifacts, their states, applicable checks, and reported blockers.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run validation for the repository.\\n2. Inspect each selected work order (WO) with the command below.\\n3. Read the work order (WO) and its returned reading manifest.\\n4. Review the governing definitions against the relevant checklists in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n5. Check that the proposed work has defined scope, verification coverage,\\n   assurance classification, and accountable humans for the required decisions.\\n6. List the blockers affecting the selected package. Keep unrelated findings\\n   separate.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl validate REPO --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint transition --target approved --json\\n```\\n\\nRead the result of each required check. A `pass` satisfies that check only;\\nit does not approve the artifacts or authorize implementation. If a required\\ncheck returns `fail` or `not_assessable`, report its identifier and stated\\nreason, then follow \\u201cIf a procedure is blocked.\\u201d Do not proceed with the\\naffected approval.\\n\\nThe last command evaluates work-order approval gates. A checkpoint-free\\n`check` only reports lifecycle context. Definition artifacts cannot be selected\\nwith `check`; their transition gates are evaluated by the previews in [Apply the selected transitions](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#apply-the-selected-transitions).\\n\\nBefore proposing a definition for approval, resolve its template placeholders.\\nIf it contains an `Open decisions` section, confirm that no unresolved decision\\nremains before setting that section to `None`. Do not remove an unresolved\\nquestion merely to make validation pass.\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected package has an explicit list of review findings\\nand approval blockers. No approval is inferred from these checks.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Resolve approval blockers](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#resolve-approval-blockers) resolves the blockers. [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision) presents the package for a\\nhuman decision.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"2ea6b051236e13dd427317722d16ce7941ef5a4eb4789f7fdf568ab3d0cae3c0\"}, {\"heading\": \"apply-the-selected-transitions\", \"title\": \"Apply the selected transitions\", \"anchor\": \"apply-the-selected-transitions\", \"start_line\": 221, \"end_line\": 270, \"content\": \"### Apply the selected transitions\\n\\n**Inputs:** The exact artifact IDs, target states, accountable human identities,\\nreviewed file digests, decisions from [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision), and clarification answers from\\n[Define the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#procedure) that must be retained in the transition reason.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Only explicitly selected definitions and work orders\\n  (WO) receive the applied state and lifecycle-history entries.\\n- **Transient working material:** The preview and apply results, including\\n  any refused transition.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Confirm that the reviewed artifact contents still match the human decision.\\n2. Select only the named transitions. Approve governing definitions before\\n   dependent work, or use one explicitly selected packet for mutually\\n   dependent definitions when the evaluator permits it.\\n3. Preview the transition command below.\\n4. Compare every selected ID, target state, actor, and reported effect with\\n   the human decision. Resolve failed gates before proceeding.\\n5. Recheck reviewed definition digests immediately before applying.\\n6. Repeat the passing command with `--apply`. Do not add related artifacts\\n   that the human did not select.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\n```\\n\\nFor each clarification below the formal-decision threshold, add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Question and confirmed answer\\\"` to both the preview\\nand apply command. Use the actual question and answer. When one artifact\\nhas several such answers, combine them into one reason value for that ID.\\nKeep each complete `ID=TEXT` value as one argument. Confirm the saved reason\\nin the resulting lifecycle entry before discarding the working notes.\\n\\nFor approval, `TARGET` is `approved`. For rejection, use `rejected` and add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Rejection reason\\\"` to both commands. Rejection is\\nterminal. For a packet, repeat `--set` and `--decision` for every explicitly\\nselected artifact. Preview and apply must contain the same assignments.\\n\\n**Completion:** The evaluator reports the actual applied transitions, or the\\nspecific refusal. A successful preview alone changes nothing.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Confirm the authorization result](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#confirm-the-authorization-result) confirms the resulting authority and readiness.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"c5215dec9f35d1a99bf6e4d658a594a31dc544131e70db49b1b8f79514e124b8\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORITY.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-scope37-20261011\\\\claude-331\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORITY.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"da7202bf8d1beee631e9b4b5ba01c04b440fbf76d200ccbccbb8781a59f88431\", \"bytes\": 16163, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"decision-rights\", \"title\": \"Decision rights\", \"anchor\": \"decision-rights\", \"start_line\": 19, \"end_line\": 80, \"content\": \"## Decision rights\\n\\nA profile describes who performs an action. A decision right describes which\\naction that person or agent is authorized to perform. Being a Human does not\\ngrant every right in this table. Being an Agent grants none by itself.\\n\\nUse the artifact's recorded ownership and any existing explicit delegation to\\nidentify the accountable human. Resolve a team name or ownership label to the\\nhuman making this decision. A proposed `owners` value, an example actor name,\\nor authorship of a draft MUST NOT assign decision authority.\\n\\nApproval, verification acceptance, and release decisions MUST be made by an\\nauthorized human. An agent MAY prepare the material and apply that human's\\nrecorded decision when authorized to do so. Applying the decision does not\\nmake the agent its author. The selected IDs, reviewed content, target states,\\nand reason MUST still match the decision at apply time.\\n\\nThe following stable IDs name rights and operations, not additional profiles.\\nAll applicable gates remain required.\\n\\nThese DR-* identifiers come from the selected evaluator's decision-right\\ncatalogue. Use the right and exact action returned by that evaluator. Legacy\\nowner labels identify recorded accountabilities; they do not create additional\\nHuman/Agent profiles or assign a decision to a person by themselves.\\n\\n| Right ID                   | Accountable profile and authority                                                                                                  | Required input                                                                                                    | Result                                                                                               |\\n|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\\n| `DR-DEFINITION-DECIDE`     | Human authorized to decide the selected definition.                                                                                | Complete definition, exact proposed state, and required checks.                                                   | Decision on that definition only.                                                                    |\\n| `DR-WO-SELECT`             | Human authorized to approve the work scope.                                                                                        | Complete governing chain, bounded work order, and assurance classification.                                       | Approval or rejection of the selected work order.                                                    |\\n| `DR-WO-START`              | Human or Agent executing the selected work under its recorded approval.                                                            | Unchanged approved scope and passing start checks.                                                                | The selected work order becomes `in_progress`.                                                       |\\n| `DR-WO-COMPLETE`           | Human or Agent executing the selected work under its recorded approval.                                                            | Completed scope, passing handoff checks, and retained evidence.                                                   | The selected work order becomes `implemented`.                                                       |\\n| `DR-VREC-PREPARE`          | Human or Agent under the approval of every selected work order.                                                                    | Exact candidate, selected work and verification contracts, retained evidence, and passing preparation checks.     | One `ready` verification record; no acceptance decision.                                             |\\n| `DR-VREC-DECIDE`           | Human authorized to accept verification for the selected candidate.                                                                | Ready record, assessed evidence, and the exact verify, reject, or supersede decision.                             | Decision on that verification record only.                                                           |\\n| `DR-DELIVERY-SELECT`       | Human authorized for the chosen integration or release-preparation path.                                                           | Exact candidate, destination, and coverage required by that path.                                                 | One path-specific instruction.                                                                       |\\n| `DR-RLS-PREPARE`           | Human authorizes the release inputs; a Human or Agent may carry out the preparation.                                               | Exact release contract, candidate, included work, verification coverage, version, and passing preparation checks. | One `ready` release record; no release decision.                                                     |\\n| `DR-RLS-DECIDE`            | Human authorized to make the release decision. release record only.                                                                |\\n| `DR-EXTERNAL-ACTION`       | Human authorized for the exact external action. A Human or Agent may execute the authorized operation.                             | Action, immutable target, destination, prerequisites, and recovery conditions.                                    | Authorization limited to that operation; the subsequent tool result records its actual effects.      |\\n| `DR-RELATED-RECORD-SELECT` | Human or Agent; no decision authority is needed for inspection.                                                                    | Exact existing artifact ID.                                                                                       | Read-only selection.                                                                                 |\\n| `DR-REMEDIATION-SCOPE`     | Human authorized to change the affected definition or work scope.                                                                  | Failed criterion and proposed bounded correction.                                                                 | Explicit authority for the revised scope.                                                            |\\n| `DR-DECISION-DISPOSE`      | Human accountable for the artifact blocked by the decision; for a deviation, the human accountable for the affected specification. | Open or deferred decision, selected option or disposition, exact reason, and any required revisit trigger.        | Recorded disposition and any declared paired-risk effect. Other blocked artifacts keep their states. |\\n\\nFor a lifecycle decision, the `--decision` value identifies its actual\\ndecision-maker. Supplying either identity does not grant\\nauthority. Preparation `--owner` values identify the preparation actor, not\\na future assurance or release decision-maker.\\n\\nFor `decide`, when an owner label differs from the actual human's identity,\\nuse `--authority-owner OWNER` only after establishing that human's authority\\nfor the exact decision. Keep `--decision ACTOR` as the actual human. The\\nevaluator checks that OWNER holds the existing decision right and records it\\nseparately as `authority_owner`. With the option absent, ACTOR must itself\\nmatch an eligible owner. The command does not authenticate either claim or\\ngrant consent. Do not infer a binding from the decision's own owners, an\\nearlier approval, Git configuration or the agent's identity.\\n\\nSilence, elapsed time, a commit, a pull request, tool execution, or a passing\\ncheck MUST NOT count as a human decision. Before using its execution grant, read [AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval](AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval). For a scope finding, read [RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked](RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"5581ca4e192b2a3847952cdb19582916ed96242a14941b1e5a8a610be996e1dd\"}, {\"heading\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"title\": \"Start the selected work\", \"anchor\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"start_line\": 70, \"end_line\": 104, \"content\": \"### Start the selected work\\n\\n**Inputs:** The approved work order (WO), actual executor identity, and\\ncurrent execution context.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** The selected work order (WO) becomes `in_progress`\\n  with a lifecycle-history entry when start succeeds.\\n- **Transient working material:** Start-check and transition results.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. If the work order (WO) is already `in_progress`, inspect the recorded start\\n   and resume unapplied work. Do not replay the start transition.\\n2. Otherwise, run start preflight under the existing approval.\\n3. Preview the transition to `in_progress` using the actual executor identity.\\n4. Apply the same transition only when preflight and the preview pass.\\n5. Inspect the resulting state.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected work order (WO) is `in_progress`, or its start\\nis blocked with an explicit reason. Existing approval covers a permitted\\nstart; no duplicate human start decision is required.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope) performs the authorized implementation.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"2275126fa8b418eccbaacb1972d0140709337470ba8d190b6566fd16854328d1\"}, {\"heading\": \"retain-the-implementation-evidence\", \"title\": \"Retain the implementation evidence\", \"anchor\": \"retain-the-implementation-evidence\", \"start_line\": 155, \"end_line\": 210, \"content\": \"### Retain the implementation evidence\\n\\n**Inputs:** The implemented changes, verification contracts (VER), required\\nrepository checks, and work-order evidence locations.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Retained evidence files:** Actual check results, review findings, their\\n  resolution, and Git-derived handoff evidence at authorized evidence paths.\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Evidence references may be added where the work order\\n  (WO) requires them. No lifecycle state changes here.\\n- **Transient working material:** A summary of remaining evidence gaps.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run every required check using its specified inputs and environment.\\n2. Retain the command arguments, working directory, runtime identity, exit\\n   status, output, and relevant file paths or digests.\\n3. Preserve failed results alongside later corrections and successful reruns.\\n4. Retain the material diff-review findings and their resolution.\\n5. Confirm that all required evidence files exist at the locations named by\\n   the work order (WO) or verification contract (VER).\\n6. Run review preflight.\\n7. Run the Git-derived handoff check with the trusted `BASE` from [Establish the execution context](EXECUTE_WORK.md#establish-the-execution-context).\\n8. Inspect its written evidence and reported findings. Correct in-scope gaps\\n   and rerun affected checks.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase review --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --from-git BASE --json\\n```\\n\\nFor ordinary files listed in the work order\\u2019s `evidence_paths`, confirm that\\neach file exists inside the repository and contains the required evidence.\\nThese files do not need a machine header. Use the supported evidence command\\nfor generated packets; do not edit their evaluator-owned binding fields.\\n\\nThe Git-derived handoff command writes retained evidence. Confirm its output\\npaths are covered by the approved preparation scope. If the evaluator calls\\nfor creating or rebinding the handoff evidence packet, use:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl evidence REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --json\\n```\\n\\nThis command also writes a file. It does not run tests or make an unsupported\\nclaim true. Rerun the required handoff check after correcting the evidence gap.\\n\\n**Completion:** Required implementation checks pass and the actual evidence\\nis retained, or the exact missing or failed checks remain visible.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Record implementation completion](EXECUTE_WORK.md#record-implementation-completion) requires passing handoff results. Verification later\\nassesses these retained files; they are not disposable planning notes.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"4cccfe03ac5fe219da997051bb91053576f18e67e834f0036f93e9856e8f12a2\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORITY.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-scope37-20261011\\\\claude-331\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORITY.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"da7202bf8d1beee631e9b4b5ba01c04b440fbf76d200ccbccbb8781a59f88431\", \"bytes\": 16163, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"authority-from-work-approval\", \"title\": \"Authority from work approval\", \"anchor\": \"authority-from-work-approval\", \"start_line\": 134, \"end_line\": 163, \"content\": \"## Authority from work approval\\n\\nWork-order approval authorizes execution of its approved scope: start,\\nimplementation, local edits and commits, required checks, evidence capture,\\ncompletion recording, and required verification-record preparation. A Human\\nand an Agent MUST use the same local approval, scope, and evidence checks.\\nContinue these operations without asking for the same permission again while\\ntheir scope and conditions still match. Record execution under the actual\\nexecutor's identity.\\n\\nApproval leaves the work order `approved` until its start transition is\\napplied. Selecting and scheduling work remain explicit. New approvals need\\nno separate execution delegation table or second start approval. A preliminary\\nmerge and live CI are not local execution requirements; CI still applies at\\nintegration and publication.\\n\\nThe grant does not include changed scope, acceptance of the result, release,\\nmerge, publication, or other external action. Reuse an existing explicit\\nauthorization for such an action only when its exact target and conditions\\nstill match.\\n\\nHistorical approvals keep their original meaning. Earlier explicit execution\\ngrants remain usable under the same scope checks. An older approval without\\nsuch a grant needs an authorized amendment for the remaining execution.\\nCurrent metadata or an actor label MUST NOT manufacture that historical grant.\\nIf the installed release cannot record the required authorization, stop the\\nremaining execution and report the missing historical grant and the lack of\\na supported amendment operation. Do not insert invented grant fields or\\nrewrite the old approval.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"a0ce00fccb8c1c8833455c14fe3ec75eaf01c597f0a1f46a24f7962ab1958f8c\"}]}]}",

The diagnostic is finished and stopped where the task requires: WO-P3-901 is `in_progress` with an accepted handoff. Both required refusals happened, and each refused attempt left no effect. The released evaluator's next step is to mark the work complete (`WO-P3-901=implemented`). I did not take it, because the task forbids completing the work.

**What was done (private test project only; Git remains authoritative):**
- **Checks before any change:** the status, client wheel and qualification candidate `50a215cd…` all match the combination report. Evaluator 0.22.1, `sandbox-projection`, `test_copy: true`.
- **New drafts:** I wrote INT-, CAP-, REQ-, SPEC-, VER- and WO-P3-901 under `docs/engineering/lifecycle-pilot/` and checked each one against its type checklist myself. The evaluator only checks template shape; it found no errors and nothing incomplete.
  - The requirement is that `greeting()` returns exactly `Hello rehearsal`.
  - The check is the `assert-greeting` tool.
  - `src/greeting.py` is in the work order's scope for inspection and running, but changing it is excluded.
- **Cypher read:** the read through the MCP tool was complete. It showed the work order's three links (`implements`, `specifications`, `verification`).
- **Stale preview:** I previewed the definition approvals, then made a real clarity fix to the VER-P3-901 draft. Applying the old preview with the *current* versions was refused with `HAG_REMOTE_STALE_PREVIEW` (409). So the cause was the changed draft, not a version mismatch. No receipt was issued, and a follow-up Cypher read showed everything still `draft` at project v15.
- **Approvals and start:** after a fresh preview with all gates passing, I approved the definitions, then the work order, as `test-owner`. Start preflight returned `ready: true`, and I started the work as `test-executor`.
- **Test:** `assert-greeting` exited 0 with `exact greeting passed`.

I corrected the path rather than widening the scope. The next preview succeeded at the same versions (18/16), which shows the refusal changed nothing.
- **Valid handoff:** all 9 changed paths since base `cda56401…` were in scope. The apply was accepted (project v19), adding the assertion record plus `handoff.json` and `WO-P3-901-handoff.md`.

**Problems and gaps (all in the report):**

- Four helper commands failed through my own usage errors, and I recovered from each one.
